Let's check: (12 - 11 + 2) = 12 - 11 + 2 = 3. And then (8) is left, and 3 * 8 = 24. But careful: We must use all four numbers exactly once. In the expression 3 * 8, the 3 is obtained from (12 - 11 + 2) and 8 is the remaining number. That uses all four numbers exactly once. So the expression is: (12 - 11 + 2) * 8. Let's check: 12 - 11 = 1, plus 2 = 3, times 8 = 24. Yes, that works.

So the expression is: (12 - 11 + 2) * 8. That is valid. We are allowed only +, -, *, / and we used them. And we used all numbers exactly once.

Thus final answer: \boxed{(12 - 11 + 2) \times 8}.

I'll output that as final answer within \boxed{}.</think>We can “hide” a 3 inside a parenthesized expression and then multiply by 8. Notice that

  12 – 11 + 2 = 3

so that

  (12 – 11 + 2) × 8 = 3 × 8 = 24.

This uses 12, 11, 2, and 8 exactly once and only the allowed operations. Therefore, one acceptable answer is

  \boxed{(12-11+2)\times8}
